{"id": "ad323c2b7c7c75aa", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T17:01:29.211244+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "dead_end", "claim": "C37 closes the fixed-cone/relaxed-projection alternative: every f=mu dist(.,K)^2/2, arbitrary closed convex K and 0<mu<=1, has true-distance normalized error <=mu product(1-mu h_i)^2, attained by a scalar quadratic. Arbitrarily many correlated active-face changes do not help. A bounded cone truncation needs an ACTUALLY active radial cap to escape.", "evidence": "# C37. All squared-distance and unsaturated cone-projection witnesses are quadratic-bounded\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED elementary joint candidate. This is a witness-class bound,\nnot an upper bound on unrestricted W. No external theorem is used as a black box.\n\nLet K be a nonempty closed convex subset of a finite-dimensional Euclidean\nspace and 0<mu<=1. Put f(x)=mu dist(x,K)^2/2. The Euclidean projection\np=P_K(x) exists uniquely. Its variational inequalities imply that\nx-P_K(x) is firmly nonexpansive; taking differences of the two minimizing\ndistance expressions shows grad f(x)=mu(x-P_K(x)). Consequently f is convex,\ndifferentiable and mu-smooth, and argmin f=K.\n\nFor any actual GD step with nonnegative physical stepsize h,\n\n    x_next=p+(1-mu h)(x-p).\n\nUsing this SAME old point p as a candidate in the next distance minimization,\n\n    dist(x_next,K)<=|1-mu h| dist(x,K).\n\nIterating gives the exact class-wide bound\n\n    2f(x_n)/dist(x_0,K)^2\n        <=mu product_(i<n)(1-mu h_i)^2.                    (1)\n\nThe right side is attained by the one-dimensional quadratic\nf(x)=mu x^2/2, K={0}. Thus allowing arbitrary dimension, arbitrary curved\nconvex K, and arbitrarily many changes of active projection face NEVER\nimproves the quadratic worst-case channel for this squared-distance class.\nThe denominator is the TRUE initial distance, not distance to an arbitrary\nchosen minimizer. If x_0 lies in K the trajectory is stationary.\n\nFor the near-critical word H_m with\nc=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c and\nH_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), (1) at mu=1 is\n\n    product_(i<n)(h_i-1)^2\n       =a^(2(2^m-1)) c^(2(2^m-m-1))\n       =(1-1/m)^(2(2^m-1)) c^(-2m).                        (2)\n\nIt is therefore exponentially small in 2^m/m, with no hidden fixed-parameter\nconstant. For other mu, (1) is still exactly bounded by the corresponding\nfull quadratic channel, without needing an asymptotic estimate.\n\nIf C is a closed convex cone containing0, the projection function\nF_C(x)=max_(p in C)(<x,p>-||p||^2/2) equals\ndist(x,C^polar)^2/2. Indeed let u=P_C(x), r=x-u. Testing the projection\ninequality at0 and2u gives <u,r>=0; testing arbitrary cone elements gives\nr in C^polar. Conversely u·(z-r)<=0 for z in C^polar, proving\nr=P_(C^polar)(x). Then F_C(x)=||u||^2/2=dist(x,C^polar)^2/2.\n\nThe same bound applies to a bounded polytope cut out of C whenever the\nactual projection onto the cone remains in the polytope at EVERY queried\npoint and terminal point. It then has exactly the same projection, value\nand trajectory, and its minimizer set has at least the same old polar\nminimizer, so the denominator convention must be checked if it is smaller.\nFor the standard truncation retaining all extreme rays of a polyhedral cone\nat positive lengths, the polar and true initial distance are unchanged, so\n(1) applies verbatim.\n\nAccordingly a bounded projection polytope can escape this obstruction only\nwhen its finite radial/face bounds alter at least one relevant projection.\nMerely using a narrow wedge or many cone faces, with all radial caps inactive,\ndoes not create a new correlated lower-bound mechanism.\n", "verifiable": false, "status": "open", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["e865afe9e0f7093c", "35f68d3b35dfe32c"], "artifact": "research/c37_squared_distance_cone_obstruction.md"}, "glossary": {"K_dist": "Nonempty closed convex minimizer set in the squared-distance construction.", "C_polar": "Polar cone, consisting of z with <z,c><=0 for every c in C.", "mu_dist": "Scalar curvature in(0,1] multiplying the squared-distance function."}}

Other planes may be present.\nAssume actual queries realize: a q-stage ending at proximal point y;\na bridge with excess D>=0; an r-stage of ordinary mass M2-1, ending\nat z=y-Dq-M2r; a bridge with excess C>=0; and a RETURNED q-stage\nof ordinary mass M3-1, ending at w=z-Cr-M3q. Here M2,M3>=1.\nThese are actual physical updates, not reset states. Heights at y,z,w\nmay be arbitrary positive values, and no endpoint is assumed minimal.\nThe named q-plane and r-plane must actually be active at their respective\nqueries; representing q as a mixture of unrelated active slopes is outside\nthis specific fixed-two-plane face policy.\n\nPut s=<q,r>, alpha=||q||^2-s, beta=||r||^2-s, and\nell(u)=<q-r,u>+o_q-o_r. The q/r active-plane conditions demand\n\n    ell(y)>=0,      ell(z)<=0,       ell(w)>=0,\n    ell(z)=ell(y)-D*alpha+M2*beta,\n    ell(w)=ell(z)+C*beta-M3*alpha.                       (15)\n\nIn any nontrivial feasible q-r-q path alpha,beta MUST be positive.\nIf alpha<=0, then beta>0 because alpha+beta=||q-r||^2>0;\nell(z)>=ell(y)+M2*beta>0 contradicts its r guard. If beta<=0,\nalpha>0 and ell(w)<=ell(z)-M3*alpha<0 contradicts its q guard.\nThus the first two guards in(15) give beta/alpha<=D/M2, and the last\ntwo give beta/alpha>=M3/C. If C=0 they directly contradict one another.\nConsequently every such fixed-two-plane return NECESSARILY obeys\n\n    D*C >= M2*M3.                                      (16)\n\nOffsets, positive affine heights, minimum-level translations or subsequent\nterminal completions cannot repair a violation: they cancel from these\nORIGINAL preterminal active-plane comparisons. Other planes only add\nguards. Define\nlambda=1+sqrt2, beta_h=1-1/(4m), c_h=lambda*(1+1/(4m)),\na_h=beta_h/c_h, H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a_h*c_h^l,H_l),\nS_l=sum H_l, b_l=1+a_h*c_h^l. Induction on the mass identity gives\n\n    1+S_l=v_h*c_h^l+(1-v_h)*2^l,  v_h=a_h/(c_h-2) in(0,1).\n\nIndeed c_h-2>1/lambda>a_h, c_h<3. For the q-r-q assignment in(17),\nD=a_h*c_h^(j+1), C=a_h*c_h^(j+2),\nM2=1+S_(j+1)>v_h*c_h^(j+1),\nM3=1+S_(j+2)>v_h*c_h^(j+2). Therefore\n\n    D*C/(M2*M3)<(a_h/v_h)^2=(c_h-2)^2<1.\n\nThis contradicts(16), even with independent affine offsets and POSITIVE\nendpoint heights. Thus this specific first-occurrence two-slope-return\nrepair cannot be instantiated on the actual inherited hierarchy. The\nexplicit return Law2 is legal because its declared physical data instead\ngive D*C/(M2*M3)=4. These two statements concern different physical\nwords; no ratio is silently substituted or reset.\n\nThis does not exclude interleaved visits with changing gradients within\na face/edge, more than two returning slopes, nonlinear curved regions,\ndifferent physical checkpoint selections, or other full smooth functions.\nIt does not give an upper bound on W_n(H_m).
